ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-15-sejm-30-1.u3	Thank you very much. We proceed with the examination of item 1 of the agenda: the report of the Committee on Agriculture and Rural Development on the government draft law amending the Act on agri-food promotion funds (prints No. 791 and 956). I would like to ask Mr Piotr Polak to present a report to the committee.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-15-sejm-30-1.u4	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! On behalf of the Committee on Agriculture and Rural Development, I present to the High House a report on the work of the Committee on Agriculture and Rural Development on the government draft law amending the Act on agri-food promotion funds, prints 791 and 956. In August 2016, the above draft law of the Marshals of the Sejm sent it to the Committee on Agriculture and Rural Development. The first reading of this draft law took place on 14 September this year. A subcommittee has been set up. The subcommittee met twice at its meetings, presented a report to the Agriculture Committee on 21 October and on 21 October the Committee on Agriculture and Rural Development decided that the High House should adopt a draft law. What is this bill about? The Act on Promotion Funds, the Act amending the Act on Agri-Food Promotion Funds, concerns the amendment of the Act of 2009. In 2009, the High House adopted this law. The Act was intended to support agricultural marketing, increased consumption and promotion of agri-food products. Nine promotion funds have been set up for this purpose. I'll zoom in and exchange those funds. These are funds to promote milk, pork meat, beef, horse meat, sheep meat, grain and cereal products, fruit and vegetables, poultry meat and the last fund - fish. High Izbo! Promotional funds are collected in funds - from the write-downs that agricultural producers bring to these products. The amount of such write-down shall be calculated on the net value sold and shall be 0,1%. net values. These are, High Izbo, not a lot of money, their number grows year by year. As of today, you can say, for last year this amount already exceeded PLN 50 million for all these funds. We started with the amount of about PLN 30 million collected from this write-down. Now this amount exceeds PLN 50 million. The Minister at the committee meeting in the explanatory memorandum, because this is a government project on the need for change, has raised the fact that it is the entities which pay the said contributions, i.e. producers, farmers, who have asked for a change in the rules of spending these funds, as well as a change in the compositions of the committees which each fund directs. I just want to remind you that every fund has a responsibility to spend this money on a special committee set up. And so far, this committee consisted of four producers' representatives - it is 9-person - four processors' representatives and one representative of the agricultural chambers. In this bill, the number of committee members does not change, but the proportions of these committees change, the government proposed it, and the committee accepted it: we increase the number of producers' representatives from four to five, and we reduce the number of processors - from four to three. Agricultural chambers shall have one representative in each committee, in each promotion fund. And it was the producers who argued that the changes should be such that the funds that are collected on these funds are devoted to specific support for agricultural marketing, increased consumption and promotion of agri-food products produced by processors from products supplied by agricultural producers. High Izbo! The work of the subcommittee was very actively attended by producers' representatives and industry associations active in the various promotion funds. That's, among other things. in this joint discussion at the subcommittee meeting, we have drawn up proposals for changes, which are the following. Firstly, the first change - I think that a very important change - is to make payments to fund the promotion of agri-food products of operators who carry out both the breeding or breeding of pigs, cattle for fattening, sheep and poultry, and in parallel the economic activity in the field of slaughter which they have just mentioned, whether of pigs or of cattle, sheep or poultry. To date, those entities which had such a closed cycle, High Izbo, that is to say, led to and carried out slaughter and processing, of this 0,1% they didn't pay off to those funds. This law, which will enter into force from the new year, these entities with this fee, includes this obligation. In accordance with the committee or the subcommittee's work, we have recognised that this is a very good change and that it is a duty for these actors, when it comes to such payments, to oblige them to do so. The next amendment aims to provide the possibility of financing or co-financing from the Fund for programmes and activities relating to fish and their processed products by the entities from which the payments to the Fund for the Promotion of Fish come. The Fish Promotion Fund is one of the smaller funds in terms of the resources being paid out and so far not all the expectations of the operators that produce fish have been met. This change will allow them to be taken into account. A further amendment is introduced in Article. 12 in the paragraph. 1 in point 6, the definition of a new task which is assigned in the Act mentioned to 9-person committees managing individual promotion funds. This new task is to develop promotion strategies for individual sectors so that promotion policies in each fund are coherent, dynamic, unambiguous and well-targeted, tailored to the needs of the sector concerned. We have heard such voices that unfortunately there have been situations that without this obligation, without this strategy, the funds from promotion funds have not always been deliberately spent. Another amendment concerns, High Izbo, a transitional period to meet the requirements for the place of birth of the pig meat from which the promotion fund will be supported by information and promotion programmes. This time limit will be 31 December 2018. This transitional period is intended to facilitate the adjustment of the meat production cycle in such a way that after 31 December 2018 support from the funds will be available for all information and promotion measures concerning pig meat from animals which were raised and slaughtered in the territory of the Republic of Poland - but the condition will also be the place of birth: these animals will have to be born in the territory of the Republic of Poland. We know that many piglets are being imported to Poland from outside our homeland, outside Poland. They are of course buried and slaughtered in Poland and by 2018 the funds from promotion funds will be available for this purpose, but after 31 December 2018 apart from the fact that they also raise and slaughter will take place in this territory, it will still be necessary to fulfil this obligation that these animals must be born in the territory of the Republic of Poland. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! As I said at the beginning, the work of the subcommittee and the commission, apart from the representatives of the Ministry, actively involved stakeholders, trade unions, because it was in response to their demands that this draft law was created. As a result of the joint discussion, as a result of a broad discussion, we have prepared a draft law - we present it to the High House - contained in print No. 956. On behalf of this committee, I ask the High House to adopt this draft law and to adopt a new law on agri-food promotion funds. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-15-sejm-30-1.u6	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I have the honour to present a position on the Act amending the Act on agri-food promotion funds, print No 791. High Izbo! The proposed amendment to the Law of 22 May 2009 responds to the proposals made by entities that make contributions to promotion funds. Operators of agri-food products have requested that their funds be paid to support agricultural marketing, increased consumption and promotion of agri-food products. The main objective of creating promotion funds is to promote marketing. The Act sets out the scope of actions which may be financed by promotion funds, i.e. exhibitions, fairs, market research and research aimed at improving the quality of products and increasing their consumption. In addition, training of producers and processors is supported. Funds may also be used to finance so-called funds. the contribution of industry organisations to one of the common agricultural policy mechanisms. The funds from the promotion fund are not public funds. They shall be managed by representatives of professional organisations who form separate management committees for each fund. The project proposes a solution whereby funds from the Funds will be used to finance and co-finance promotion programmes eligible for support from the European Union. The proposed statutory amendment sets out the requirements for the agri-food product at the same time. Generally speaking, such a product must be manufactured in the territory of the Republic of Poland. High Izbo! In order to increase the impact of agri-food producers on the way funds are spent, it is proposed that fund managers increase the number of people from four to five to producers and reduce the number of processors in these committees from four to three, which will have a significant impact on the greater representation of producers. Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I would like the High Sejm to adopt a law proposed by the government, print No. 791. Thank you very much. ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-15-sejm-30-1.u9	Thank you very much, sir. I would like to invite Mr Jarosław Sachaika, Kukiz Club15.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-15-sejm-30-1.u10	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Kukiz Club15, I am pleased to present my position on the government draft law amending the Act on agri-food promotion funds. The main objective of setting up funds is to achieve an increase in consumption of Polish agri-food products. Funds may finance a wide range of activities such as participation in fairs and exhibitions and their organisation, market research, research and development to improve the quality of products. These measures are consequently intended to lead to the basic objective which I have already mentioned, namely the increase in consumption of products. Has this objective been achieved for 7 years the current law has been in force? As the CUS data shows, not necessarily. The consumption of agri-food products varies but is not significant in the long term. A stronger trend is seen in the consumption of poultrymeat, the consumption of which is increasing year by year, mainly due to price. However, the opposite situation is in the case of beef, which has a downward trend in consumption. According to the justification of the project, the reason why amendments to the present law were decided were numerous requests for a change in the functioning of promotion funds. The conclusion is one - the money was wrongly spent. It should be considered whether the changes proposed in the project are sufficient to restore the original idea and direction to the promotion funds. In my opinion, the bill tries to fix the problem to a very limited extent, fragmentedly. A comprehensive and in-depth look at the current law and the effects of the solutions proposed in the project is needed. During my work in committee, I have tabled proposals for amendments, which have not met with the approval of representatives of the Ministry, which would certainly contribute to improving the spending of funds and the promotion of Polish agri-food products. The first amendment aims to focus programmes and actions undertaken by promotion funds exclusively on the promotion of products produced and derived from ingredients manufactured in the territory of the Republic of Poland. This purpose is aimed at reducing the amount of ingredients in a processed product other than from the territory of the Republic of Poland from 25 registered in the project to 10, which still seems to be a very large share of raw materials from abroad. Since the funds collected in funds come exclusively from Polish farmers, they should be entirely devoted to the promotion of Polish products rather than Greek, Spanish, Italian products. The second arrangement is intended to ensure that only persons representing natural persons who have been resident in the territory of the Republic of Poland for at least 5 years or who represent legal persons in which more than half of the shares belong to natural persons who have resided in the territory of the Republic of Poland for at least 5 years. I will repeat the words often used by Mrs Szydło and Deputy Prime Minister Morawiecki: capital has nationality. In my opinion, the far-reaching caution of the Ministry and some of the parliamentarians is not indicated in this case. Either we are in favour of the full support of Polish products produced by Polish companies, or we are satisfied with half-measures, considering the risk that the solutions proposed by the government will not be sufficient to ensure the proper functioning of the funds, and - as is the case today - promotion funds will exist, they will spend money to farmers, without harming anyone, but also helping. Is an institution acting in such a form therefore necessary to anyone at all? This is a decision that I would like to ask your Members to consider while making the amendments I have discussed. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-15-sejm-30-1.u11	Thank you very much, sir. And I invite Mrs Ewa Lieder, the Modern Club.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-15-sejm-30-1.u12	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! Funds for the promotion of agri-food products were created under the May 2009 Act. Eight of them were created: milk, pork, beef, horse, sheep, grain of cereals, cereal products, fruit and vegetables and poultry meat and fish. Support for promotional or information campaigns may be requested by associations of producers of the agricultural sector concerned. Participation in this mechanism allows organisations to obtain a refund of up to 80% net amounts of costs actually incurred for the implementation of promotion and information activities - up to 50% appropriations come from the European Union budget, 30% from the national budget - the remaining 20% represents the organisation's own contribution. It is a good idea that funds from agri-food promotion funds should be allocated to information activities and promotion of products produced by Polish producers and processors making payments to these funds. The idea is not that the funds should support products from imports, but not enough to promote products from Polish farmers, which was signalled by farmers' organisations and trade unions. Funds are intended to cover promotion measures for agri-food products produced in Poland and, in the case of processed products, promotion may apply to those used for production not more than 25% ingredients originating outside Poland, mainly food additives. Of course, we view this as a positive change. In the course of the work on the Law adopted in May 2009, the Ministry of Agriculture and Rural Development was in receipt of requests for the creation of further promotion funds, inter alia. Ecological Food Promotion Fund - Eko Mazovia Eco Food Manufacturers and Processors Association or Beekeeping Promotion Fund - Polish Bee Association. At the first reading of the project, there was a voice on the difficulty in raising funds for the niche related to the promotion of vegetables. I am referring here to the speech of a representative of the Association of Producers of Paprika of the Republic of Poland. Mr Bogucki himself mentioned the appropriate proportions applied to each of these funds. It is important not to forget about the promotion of organic food, but also about the promotion of products that are not mainly of animal origin. Organic food has support for European Union funds, but it is much less money than those with promotion funds. The main task of organic farming is to ensure the quality and protection of the entire environment in which agriculture operates, to produce high quality foods in a very diverse environment and to ensure the welfare of animals. When introducing amendments to the Act, it is worth considering the role of funds in promotion in the context of ecology and environmental protection, as well as what should be promoted and in what proportions as part of state policy. Let us not forget that ethical, social and environmental aspects are increasingly important for Polish consumers. Today's decisions will also have an impact on the marketing strategies used on the Polish market for organic food. As regards the change in the membership of the Promotion Funds Management Boards, which many Members have mentioned here, i.e. the increase in the number of producers' representatives from four to five and the reduction in the number of processors' representatives from four to three, this should be summarised in such a way that the aim for producers and processors is the same, i.e. selling as much as possible, and it is important that any changes in this area do not interfere with liquidity in the right, beneficial for the promotion of Polish food management funds. But I don't think that's the right direction. Despite these comments, Poselski Club Modern is in favour of further work in the committee on the draft law. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-15-sejm-30-1.u14	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present on behalf of the Parliamentary Club of the Polish People's Party a position regarding changes to the Act on agri-food promotion funds. Both the rapporteur and the previous speakers reminded that the law was passed in May 2009 and that it was a natural response to demand in terms of promoting Polish food, and not only was it limited to promoting Polish food in Poland, but it was also promoting Polish food in export to external markets, because the issue of increasing the consumption of food products in Poland is hope, of course, also of farmers and processors, because we want consumers not to arrive, but is decreasing. Unfortunately, that's the demographic. And what I thought at the time was the essence of setting up these promotion funds, however, is mainly the creation of a system of product promotion in exports. And I do not agree with Mr Sachaika that this money was wrongly spent because, first, the rapporteur pointed out that in 2010 the impact on these funds was very little more than PLN 30 million, but already in 2015 it exceeded 50. Not because the discipline of the flow of these funds has increased, but above all because production, sales and exports have increased. And this export in 2007 was in the range of EUR 10 billion, and in 2015 it already exceeded EUR 25 billion, EUR 25 billion with a positive balance sheet of over EUR 7 billion. So these slogans are important: promotion of the domestic product, about national economic aspects, but in a situation where more than 32% of Polish food is exported and we are still a country looking for new markets, it is worth to use this term sparingly, so to speak, to operate. Once again, in 2007, outside the European Union, the countries to which we were selling were less than 27. In 2015, it was already more than 71 countries from all over the world outside the European Union. And it would be good for this dynamic to continue. What bothered me about this year's GUS data is that in the first half of this year we have had a decline in export dynamics of agri-food products for the first time in 10 years, and this is a decrease of more than 6%. In 2015, exports for the first half of the year had a positive balance of EUR 3.8 billion, this year - only EUR 3.4 billion. So there is some concern on the market related to export and promotion of Polish food. I'm not questioning the proposed changes. I wouldn't even be afraid, unlike Mr Aichler, of remodeling the board of directors. The period of 7 years of operation of promotion funds has allowed considerable experience and farmers, and processors, so it is possible to make an evaluation and possibly some adjustments in order to make export dynamics much higher. My concern, however, is the provision, the proposal to reduce promotion funds to promote only Polish food products in a situation where these promotion funds have also opened the way for us to reach for funds from the European Union budget. Not from the Rural Development Programme, but from these additional funds to promote European food in the world. At a time when Poland did not have these promotion funds, our entities, our processors' organisations and producers did not apply for these EU funds. When the funds came in, we became the leader in using EU funds to promote European food, of course, by promoting Polish food first and foremost. So I think it would be worthwhile for the government to examine whether this would limit access to EU promotional measures. If it doesn't limit, I'm in favour of getting those records in place. In conclusion, after seven years, I clearly want to say that promotion funds [[♪ The bell ♪]] they have played their part, but as all this requires ongoing analysis, evaluation and correction. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-15-sejm-30-1.u15	Thank you very much, sir. I invite Mr Ireneusz Gaina, the Free and Solidarnia Members' Circle.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-15-sejm-30-1.u16	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Free and Solidarnia Circle, I have the honour to present a position towards the government draft law amending the Act on agri-food promotion funds, prints No. 791 and 956. The aim of the promotion funds is to promote marketing, increased consumption and promotion of agri-food products. To this end, the Act sets out activities: participation in exhibitions and fairs, conducting market research on individual products, research and development to improve the quality of products and to increase their consumption, as well as training and participation in the work of specialised producers and processors, which may be financed by promotion funds. The funds collected in the accounts of the funds shall be collected in the form of a tribute to the marketing of agri-food products paid by producers. Therefore, it is impossible to disagree with the fact that if someone is giving money, he expects a greater influence on what happens to the fruits of his work. We believe that taking into account the demands of stakeholders in this area is a very good decision. In view of the changes made to the rules on financing and co-financing of promotion and information programmes, it is a good solution to introduce transitional provisions that will allow programmes operating on the date of entry into force of the amendment to the Act to benefit from support under the existing rules. Management committees shall decide on the financial allocation of promotion funds for specific tasks included in the annual financial plans. Since the amendment of the Act will change the proportion of the number of members of the agri-food promotion funds management committees by reducing the number of members selected by producer organisations and processors in favour of the former, it is necessary to select new compositions of the management committees. It is important in this situation to define clear criteria and how to choose. In our view, the new arrangements proposed by the Minister for Agriculture and Rural Development regarding the procedure for submitting candidates for the members of the agri-food promotion funds management committee and the way in which they are selected meet this criterion. Your Marshal, High Izbo, the Free and Solidarni Circle will support the proposed amendment of the law. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-15-sejm-30-1.u34	Mrs. Marshal! High Seym! Minister! I have the honour to present a report by the Committee on Agriculture and Rural Development on the Government's draft law amending the Act on Agricultural and Farm Animal Insurance from print No. 469. The aim of the project is to improve the agricultural insurance system by providing farmers with wider access to agricultural insurance with State subsidies to agricultural insurance premiums by introducing increased tariff rates. The project entered the Sejm on April 26th. and was addressed to the Committee on Agriculture and Rural Development on 29 April. The first reading of the above draft took place at the committee meeting on 9 June this year, where an extraordinary subcommittee was set up to consider the above draft. Sub-committee at meetings on 21 July and 5 September this year. considered the draft and produced a report. The subcommittee's report was examined on 13 September this year. The Commission has adopted a report which is being discussed today. During the work of the subcommittee, proposals for amendments were tabled, including proposals for amendments. concerning the new wording of the mouth. 5 in Article 5, paragraph 1 is replaced by the following: 3. The new wording of the paragraph. 5 concerned the introduction of a new sixty-day period during which the risk of agricultural drought, i.e. the period from 21 March to 20 May, will be monitored. The introduction of a new sixty-day period is linked to the monitoring of agricultural drought of water resources from the winter period, which contribute to the enrichment of soil information in the first decades of the vegetation period. Adding a sixty-day period will improve the agricultural drought monitoring system. Following the introduction of the new sixty-day period, a new definition of drought has also been given, which will take into account the monitoring of drought during the period from 21 March. The wording of the definition: drought will therefore mean damage caused by a drop in the climatic water balance below the value specified for each crop species and soil during any period from 21 March to 30 September. A proposal from the Polish Insurance Association was also addressed to the subcommittee, which considered that certain provisions of the Act could restrict the insurance offer to agricultural producers and could therefore increase the State's expenditure on direct aid to injured agricultural producers in the future. As a rule, printing project 469 did not cause much controversy. The Committee on Agriculture and Rural Development adopted the report as a result of the subcommittee's report. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-15-sejm-30-1.u35	Thank you very much, sir. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-15-sejm-30-1.u36	Mrs. Marshal! High Seym! Minister! I have the honour to present the position of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice on the Government's draft amendment to the Law on agricultural and livestock insurance, print Nos 469 and 838. It concerns the improvement of the agricultural insurance system by providing farmers with a wider availability of agricultural insurance with State subsidies for insurance premiums. Drought, hail, floods, drowning, frosts, heavy rain or other natural disasters are a constant threat to agricultural production. After each occurrence of massage of these phenomena, the problem of insurance returns. The victims then expect State aid. Insurance companies, too, do not want to cover any disaster that can cover a larger area of the country because they are afraid of financial losses. Unfortunately, a large proportion of farmers are not insured because contributions are high and they simply cannot afford it, so they take such risks. In 2015, 144 000 of them benefited from crop insurance. farmers and 426 bought livestock insurance. The draft law on the amendment of the Act on agricultural and livestock insurance is a response to problems arising from the low level of agricultural insurance in Poland. In a situation of various kinds of emergency events, the absence of such insurance causes, on the one hand, significant losses for farmers, on the other hand, the need for exceptional State aid in this respect. In order to ensure an adequate level of insurance protection for agricultural producers, a comprehensive and universal insurance system should be established, taking into account farmers' profitability and often lack of it, and the budgetary capacity of the country. The insurance model must include three stakeholders: farmers wishing to be insured, a government that secures the State's budget for insurance subsidies, so that they are viable and bearable by the farmer, and insurance companies to be a partner in the scheme. The aim of the amendment is to ensure better protection of agricultural production by increasing the insurance cover of agricultural crops, which will lead to an increase in the number of insurance contracts concluded and a reduction in the risk of production. The draft law proposes to increase the tariff rates for agricultural producers to receive subsidies from the state budget from the current 3.5%, 5% and 6% crop insurance values up to 9% , with the possibility of increasing them to 12% and 15% for crops on weaker soils where the risk of crop losses is significantly higher. Such tariff rates are intended to ensure that an agricultural insurance contract can be concluded with State aid also on these land, which will benefit farmers. Currently, a significant proportion of the tariff rates exceed 6% of the amount of agricultural insurance, which affects the lack of adjustment of State subsidies to insurance premiums. The current rules only allow for state grant insurance for fruit and vegetable crops with rates of insurance above 6% insurance. The draft law introduces preferences for the conclusion by farmers of a package agreement of all risks to which a surcharge of up to 65% of the premium is applied, provided that the proposed maximum tariff rates are not exceeded. Where an insurance premium is fixed above 9%, 12% and 15% the aid would apply only to this level, while the remaining part of the contribution would be paid by the agricultural producer in full. In concluding single risk insurance contracts or in combination several selected risks after exceeding the above tariff rates, the aid will not be granted. And here, Your Marshal, in this matter, the Law and Justice Club is making amendments that will somehow allow for subsidies and a solution to this problem. I'm making these amendments.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-15-sejm-30-1.u37	Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-15-sejm-30-1.u38	As regards livestock insurance, aid would be granted to agricultural producers of 65% contributions where the insurance undertaking determines the total rates of animal insurance not exceeding 0,5% the amount of insurance. Implementation of the Act requires securing in the state budget for 2017 an increase in the financial resources from PLN 200 million this year up to PLN 926 million. In 2020 this would be a total of PLN 1621 million. This would therefore be an eightfold increase compared to the current amount of subsidies. The changes made to the insurance scheme would apply from 1 January 2017. ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-15-sejm-30-1.u46	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! This amendment is already one, I don't know if it's 10, 9 or 10, from the beginning, since this system has been functioning. The catalog of these events is wide - from phenomena that are marginal, such as: heavy rain, to phenomena that are common, such as the failure of hail, the failure of spring frosts or the very common drought disaster in Poland. This system allows farmers to insurance their crops and livestock against precisely these events - the payment of contributions, insurance in commercial insurance companies and, in the event of loss of yield, income, receiving compensation. And that's one way to protect yourself from natural disasters. Another method is technical protection, such as sprinklers, anti-haul grids, irrigation systems. In fact, farmers have the opportunity to benefit from a system that is more efficient, cheaper or more efficient. These two systems operate widely in rural areas, but not as widely as we all wish, which is unfortunately not protected from most crops. Many believe that it is a better security than technical protection, many cannot afford it because it is usually more expensive, so they are insured in insurance companies, and there are those who do not cover themselves or apply technical safeguards and expect state aid in extreme situations. Let me remind you that the crop loss must be well above... about 70% or a 30% loss of income. So there is widespread dissatisfaction. An alternative is the insurance system - an insurance system that plays a key role in helping farmers in tragic situations in many countries - but it also has its limitations. We all know very well that they are collapsing here as if three expectations and opportunities - on the one hand, we have the budget of the state which determines this every year, has some amount, and, as a rule, it is not enough to support the system, i.e. to add to the contributions, there are farmers themselves who, in the event of a natural disaster, would like to receive compensation probably such the same as the loss incurred, and as a rule it is not, and there are insurance companies in this system who would like to receive as much contributions as possible and pay as little compensation as possible. This system has evolved over the years. Or we changed the possibility of co-financing in the form of a surcharge, from 50 to today 65% We either raised the maximum rates proposed by insurance companies, increased budget opportunities, and often changed the rules themselves. This Act today is a law on change of rules, because there are package insurances that notabene have already been. At that time we considered that these package insurances, because of the high potential cost of insurance companies, caused this system to be uncommon, and then, a few years ago, we gave it up for insurance from one risk. Today we are returning to this package insurance and for this reason there are some doubts that this system will certainly be a more efficient, better functioning system, because it really depends on how many farmers will participate in this system. The more common it will be, the cheaper it will be in the form of contributions, because also those who are not sure that there will be a risk on their farm. Today, it is usually insured by those who have almost such certainty, and insurance companies are prepared to pay compensation, thus raising their premiums or not submitting an offer. Time will show whether this amendment that we are discussing today will meet farmers' expectations in this respect, whether it will make this system more universal and thus make it more efficient and cheaper for farmers. There is probably a legitimate concern that this is not a revolutionary change that will quickly change and improve the operation of this system, but it is more an evolutionary change, which will rather be a pilot or a reason to observe the operation of this system. [[♪ The bell ♪]] in the following years, assess its effectiveness and perhaps prepare an even better revision. But because this system, or agricultural insurance, is very important from the point of view of Polish farms, we will support this project and continue to work on it in committees. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-15-sejm-30-1.u47	Thank you very much, sir. Please, Mr Ireneusz Gains, near Free and Solidarnia.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-15-sejm-30-1.u48	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Freedom and Solidarnia Circle, I present my position on the Government's draft law amending the Act on Agricultural and Farm Animal Insurance, prints Nos 469 and 838. The cautious always insured - this old saying seems to know everyone. Agricultural activity as a commercial activity is subject to one of the biggest risks, unpredictable, related to unexpected meteorological phenomena and natural disasters. Today, when the majority of investments due to competition are carried out with a minimum provision to cover the expected losses, it is very important that the largest number of farmers engaged in agricultural activities should be covered by insurance. Currently, most of the contracts concluded by farmers with insurers cover single risks and because of adverse rates do not cover insurance against the most serious and most common risks of drought. The solution proposed in the draft law for insurance subsidies sold in the so-called. risk packages seem to be good for both sides. The policyholder will be able to spread the costs into more components and the insured will be fully protected from possible losses. The high grant of such insurance should make it relatively cheap. The draft law is a burden on the budget, but it must be borne in mind that the State as an institution is obliged to make social commitments and ensure citizens' security. The lack of insurance available due to the high cost of the insurance premium is particularly severe for small farms whose profitability is relatively low. These farms belong to less wealthy farmers and therefore the risk of insurance against non-insurance is double negative. Farms with low economic potential may need many years to rebuild the situation before adverse circumstances. For many of them it may be unbearable, especially if the defeat happens again. Aid to obtain insurance and, consequently, to protect less viable holdings against bankruptcy in the event of a risk has another advantage. It is common opinion that Polish farmers produce good, healthy food. It is therefore better to support a well-known supplier than to import food from an uncertain source and to adopt permanent beneficiaries on the shoulders of the budget and social assistance. As the balance of the State and its citizens, support for agricultural insurance will pay off. During the public consultation it was suggested that the most expensive insurance, such as drought insurance, should cover 90% of the subsidy rate. However, in accordance with the European Union Guidelines on State aid in the agricultural and forestry sectors and in rural areas for the period 2014 to 2020 the aid intensity shall not exceed 65% of the cost of insurance premiums. The amendment to the Act will ensure greater availability of agricultural and animal insurance by introducing increased tariff rates to which State subsidies will be adjusted. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The Free and Solidarni Circle supports the bill submitted. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-15-sejm-30-1.u78	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The Law and Justice Club believes that a government project from print No.952 should be adopted. The implementation of tax returns on trade tax should be suspended and enforcement of that tax should be suspended by the end of 2017, by 1 January 2018, i.e. until the European Commission or the European Court of Justice has settled the case. On this occasion, it is worth explaining some of the issues that have been raised in this public debate and which need to be clarified more widely. Whether this form of tax is public aid raises serious doubts among Members of the Law and Justice, namely because the proposed construction is a construction of the level progression, i.e. the kind of progression that does not create differentiation between taxpayers. There is no difference between taxpayers who are in a comparable situation, i.e. in a situation where selective public aid could be considered to exist, the measure is, in our view, a measure in general. Taxpayers at the same level of income are taxed exactly the same. The higher rate shall be calculated only on the excess tax base over the threshold setting the rate. In short, there is no distinction between individual taxpayers. There is also no zero tax rate. There is a free amount, but it concerns all entities covered by the Act. 0.8% is a monthly income of PLN 17 to 170 million, and 1.4% is only a surplus of PLN 170 million over this revenue. I understand that both Members of the Modern Platform and Members of the Civic Platform will agree that large-income companies have greater opportunities for tax optimization, achieve economies of scale, have more productivity, we only draw a different conclusion, because we draw from this the conclusion that, through the possibility of tax autonomy of the individual Member States of the European Union, we should strive to use all the instruments that will result in social redistribution, financial redistribution and exploitation of the potential of large players in the trading market as a result of tax solutions. Level progression - this is also worth explaining - only applies to revenues. Income can, of course, affect the size of the company, but it does not have to, so it is that in short periods very high revenues due to the seasonality of the business can achieve different types of business, which are normally business according to the definition of a small and medium-sized enterprise. It must also be said that the Act does not exempt companies from tax because of: a certain size, sector, legal form, date of formation of the company, membership of a group of companies, difficult situation of the company or export-related activities. There is therefore no formal selectivity in the case of the project that we have presented, and in our view it is doubtful that there is also a finding of factual selectivity, in short, what the European Commission is accusing us of and investigating at the moment, i.e. state aid. We want to be treated in the same way as other countries, for example. France, which uses slightly different solutions. We do not want to be compared to Hungary because there is no time for this. But there are important differences that make this comparison a complete abstraction. This is not the same construction as the Hungarian construction. We just want to know that... [[♪ The bell ♪]] That is to say, we are in the position that the Member States in tax matters have autonomy, and we have used this autonomy. We will be evaluated by the Commission, perhaps by the Court. Let's see what happens. For now, we want to suspend this tax.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-15-sejm-30-1.u96	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Polish Free and Solidarnia Circle, I present my position on the Government's draft law amending the Act on Retail Tax, print No. 952, and the report of the Public Finance Commission regarding this bill, print No. 1007. The government project introduces a provision whereby the provisions of the Retail Tax Act of 6 July 2016 would apply to sales revenue achieved from 1 January 2018 and would not apply to retail sales revenue achieved between September 2016 and the end of December 2017. As we can read in the explanatory memorandum, the amendment follows from the European Commission's decision of 19 September 2016 that it initiated a detailed investigation into the retail tax, while issuing an order obliging Poland to suspend its application until its analysis by the Commission has been completed. One of the main objectives of introducing a retail tax, so-called commercial tax, it was a level playing field between small and medium Polish trading companies and large foreign trading networks, which often effectively avoided taxation in Poland. For example, the report of the Republican Foundation also showed that in the same tax system there are large commercial networks on the Polish market, which in 2011 paid PLN 241 million in one case tax, in another PLN 600 million in another case, in the case of another very large network not even EUR 1 gr of CIT was paid, while another commercial network in which millions of Poles make purchases received as much as PLN 14 million of tax refund. In such a situation, it is necessary to take steps not only to bring retail tax into line with European Union law, where the provisions of the Act are found to be incompatible with the State aid rules, but also to reflect on the current highly imperfect construction of CIT. It is worth noting that the European Commission does not question Poland's right to decide on the level of taxation or the purpose of individual taxes and charges, including those relating to the so-called. commercial tax. Therefore, the government's proposal to cease the application of the law seems more reasonable than its total repeal, all the more so as the current construction of the commercial tax is socially acceptable. It is the result of a compromise reached after long negotiations with numerous representatives of the trade industry. I took part in the meeting at the Chancellery of the President of the Council of Ministers, where many Polish entrepreneurs from small and medium-sized commercial networks participated in the meeting of the Parliamentary Group for the Support of Entrepreneurship and Economic Patriotism. So that this form of law was preceded by long public consultations. A similar compromise should be reached where it is necessary to propose a different structure of the tax. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Members of the Free and Solidarni Circle will support the government bill amending the Retail Tax Act. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-15-sejm-30-1.u124	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Free and Solidarnia Circle, I have the honour to present my position on the presidential draft law amending the Act on Pensions and Pensions from the Social Insurance Fund and some other laws. When the retirement age was increased in 2012, many Poles were very dissatisfied. Suddenly there was a change that hit many Polish citizens. The increase and equalisation of the retirement age in Poland in 2012 was not preceded by an appropriate public debate. The main purpose of the law was the economic balance, which was translated by the rulers, among others. Poland's demographic situation. In determining the retirement age, the biological circumstances of the ageing process have not been taken into account, in which the border should be determined primarily by the age at which man is no longer capable of fully efficient work. This important factor has been taken into account in the presidential bill. The contested law also retains the second important factor to be taken into account in determining the retirement age, i.e. the proportion between the insurance period and the benefit collection period. It should also be stressed that the situation on the labour market and the general socio-economic situation of Poland do not justify an increase in the basic retirement age. I agree with the designers that Poles, despite their long lives, are no longer capable of fully efficient work. Therefore, the new concept of the Act restores the retirement age of 60 years for women and 65 years for men until 2012. It should be noted that the change resulting from the reduction in retirement age does not imply retirement at the age of 60 in the case of women and 65 in the case of men. According to the Supreme Court's ruling, reaching the retirement age cannot be the sole reason to terminate the employee's employment contract. Therefore, Polish citizens can, in accordance with the amendment, decide for themselves whether or not to continue their work. In addition to the restoration of the retirement age, the contested law also eliminates a partial pension institution which, due to the reduction in the retirement age, becomes pointless. In view of the restoration of the retirement age of at least 60 years for women and 65 years for men, the Council of Ministers undertakes to review the functioning of the pension system every 3 years and to submit information on the effects of the Act to the Sejm of the Republic of Poland by 31 October of the following calendar year. This very important provision will allow to monitor the possible effects of the amendment on an ongoing basis. The argument for reducing the retirement age is also the Polish tradition related to family support. Although the law imposes a burden on the state budget, its positive social impact and the reduction of social security expenditure, social assistance and health benefits are undeniable. I am pleased that the draft law was so widely discussed and that there was no shortage of public speaking during the process. The Free and Solidarni Circle asks for further proceedings of the law. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-15-sejm-30-1.u180	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I would like to begin by thanking you for all the votes that have been cast in the discussion on the presidential bill amending the Social Insurance Fund pension law. I would like to thank you for the questions raised, because they concern, you can say, the key elements of the pension system. In view of the number of questions, I would like to comment on the main points of this discussion. During the debate, there was a question of pension risk in the context, it can be said that there is a difference between the risk of reaching retirement age and the risk of incapacity for work. It can be said that the characteristics of the risk of reaching retirement age or pension risk are conventional. This conventionality means otherwise incompetence, it means that we, either by sending or not sending the insured person a pension, do not individually assess his health, we do not individually assess his ability to work, we do not assess whether he has already individually fulfilled the condition of reaching an old age or not, we merely determine, in a sense, average this condition, we determine this condition in general for the whole of society. So from this point of view, the elements that we justify as a government the support of the presidential bill reducing retirement age are important. I am referring to the element of the security that says that, by setting this conventional limit of the retirement age, we must take into account the average age after which insurance loses its ability to work, while, of course, maintaining this difference as regards the mere assessment of the fulfilment of the condition of reaching the retirement age. Other elements that have also been highlighted in the official position of the Council of Ministers: this retirement age must be set at such a level as to maintain the proportion between the period of insurance and the period of retirement. According to the Government, these proportions are maintained in a situation where the retirement age would return to the level in force by the end of 2012. [[Almost 1 to 1.]] The issue of health status arises naturally during this discussion and is also related to the content of Convention No 102 on minimum social security standards. I remember exactly the contents of the article. Article 26 (2) (b) 2 of this Convention, which provides that the retirement age limit should not exceed 65 years of age, unless - and this is the only condition permitted by the Convention - the health situation of society is that most of the population, it can be said, retains the ability to work beyond that age limit. Thus, from the official position of the Council of Ministers, this condition has not been met in our country. [[And what year is this convention?]] The convention is from 1952, and I would like to say that we have... [[It's gonna be 100 years soon.]] In general, as we read in the statement of reasons for the bill of 11 May 2012, the law that raised the retirement age, we will conclude that you have overlooked the issue of the International Labour Organisation Convention at all. After reading the explanatory memorandum, we conclude that this is a purely economic justification, that you followed only economic considerations, i.e. to reduce expenditure on benefits. [[That's not true.]] You have not pointed out that Poland is covered by Convention No 102, including Article. 26b that Poland has not ratified Convention 128 of the International Labour Organisation, which gives more scope to apply exceptions to this limit of 65 years as retirement age, among others. This is the element that you referred to in the explanatory memorandum of the bill of 11 May 2012, i.e. the financial situation of the insurance fund. So that this is not actually a link to a government that takes into account the international commitments of our country. [[This time, once.]] [[And your health hasn't changed?]] Other issues that emerged during the discussion. He returns, of course, and has returned several times in the form of a question asked by several Members of the Polish People's Party, the problem of the alleged increase in the retirement age of farmers. You have the right to ask questions, so let me ask you a question, too. [[The Minister doesn't have that right.]] The presidential bill amending the Social Insurance Fund's pension law provides for a reduction in the retirement age from a target of 67 years, this ceiling will be reached in 2040 for both sexes, up to 60 years for women and 65 years for men. This solution, the establishment of the presidential project, concerns both persons covered by the general pension scheme and farmers, persons covered by the farmers' pension scheme. The project as it left the committee also provides for the maintenance of a provision which was introduced by the coalition forces of the Civic Platform and the PSL, which provides that early retirement is possible, i.e. When a woman reaches 55 years of age and a male of 60 years of age is also satisfied with certain internship requirements, she will be maintained until the end of 2017. I ask you to answer the question: To what extent does the presidential project provide for an increase in the retirement age, since it assumes a reduction in the retirement age for all insured people... [[Voice from the Chamber: This is an amendment of the PSL.]] ...including farmers, as well as the behaviour or respect for the decision made by the State in the previous term of the Sejm, to terminate the possibility for farmers to retire at the end of 2017 after the age of 55 by women and 60 by men? In the course of the discussion there were also threads on the financial situation of the social security system or the Social Insurance Fund. This is, of course, an expression of the concern of all countries for the condition or balance of the public finance system, which is understandable. I would like to say that caring for the financial balance of the social security system should not only mean caring that the expenditure on benefits should not be too high. This concern should also mean that the income of the Social Insurance Fund should be sufficiently high. [[What do you do with income?]] Here's what I'm gonna say. [[I don't think you did.]] I am talking about changes in the area of labour law, taking care of, you can say, the application of appropriate employment bases. [[That's not true.]] A number of actions, which the government first took, are aimed at ordering the situation on the labour market. I would like to say that today's pension-setting mechanism, which is currently in force - which does not apply to farmers - was introduced in 1999 as a mechanism known as the formula of the defined contribution. This is a mechanism that correlates the amount of the pension, directly, both with the amount of contributions, and this is a consequence of the revenue generated, as well as the length of the period of payment of social security contributions and the moment of the decision to terminate the working life and retire. If I had a short, synthetic answer to the question of the amount of future pensions after the entry into force, after the adoption and entry into force of the presidential bill amending the Social Insurance Fund pension law, I would say that it will be very different, because it will depend on these three factors, these three parameters that I mentioned earlier. This decision was taken in 2007. This decision entered into force, of course the decision of the German Parliament, 5 years after the adoption of the law. This decision whether or not the decision entered into force meant that 5 years after the law was passed it began gradually, breaking it down for many years, increasing the retirement age to 67 years. I would like to say that the decision that was taken in Germany in this regard was a decision preceded by a long-term social debate, a long-term expert debate. The question arises as to whether these standards were preserved when deciding to increase the retirement age in Poland. One could say that the topic, the problem of increasing the retirement age or its announcement appeared in October 2011 after the election was won by the Civic Platform coalition and the Polish People's Party. This period during which a draft law was being developed to raise retirement age was very short. You are calling for a referendum. On behalf of the Joint Committee on Agriculture and Rural Development and the Committee on Economic Affairs and Development, I have the honour to present a report to the Committee on the Prevention of Unfair Use of Contractual Advantage in Trade in Agricultural and Food Products, print No 790. The aim of this bill is to eliminate unfair commercial practices from the supply chain of agricultural raw materials and food. The draft law applies only to suppliers of agricultural and food products due to the specific nature of the agri-food industry consisting in the production of goods which are characterised by a specific shelf-life date, which does not exist in other industries. The joint committees gathered on the day... This project was addressed by the Marshal of the Sejm on 14 September 2016 to the joint committees, the Committee on Agriculture and Rural Development and the Committee on Economic Affairs and Development. The joint committees referred the draft to the subcommittee chosen by these committees. The extraordinary subcommittee, after consideration at its meetings on 22 September, 21 October and 3 November 2016 of the Government's draft law against unfair exploitation of the contract advantage in trade in agricultural and food products, presented the attached draft to the Sejm. I would add that, in the work of both the committee and the subcommittee, the Members and the Social Party did not question the need for the law to prevent unfair exploitation of the contractual advantage in the marketing of agricultural and food products. Of course, it was not without discussion, without comments. The discussion took place, as I said before, in the subcommittee and in the committee. Seven amendments were adopted during the process, including: I'm sorry. I'm sorry. I'm sorry. Amendment 1 concerned clarification of the definition. Draft Print Act No 790 proposes the following changes to the definition: supplier, i.e. supplier, is understood to be an entrepreneur who manufactures or processes agricultural or food products or sells them for consideration to the buyer and the buyer - that is to say, an entrepreneur who directly or indirectly acquires agricultural or food products from the supplier for the purpose of sale, resale or processing. Amendment 2 concerned the correction of the reference. As regards the third amendment, it concerned the clarification of the characteristics of the verification activities. The point was that the director of the field branch of the agency competent according to the place of residence of the buyer carries out activities to verify compliance with the obligation to conclude contracts. Another amendment, the fourth amendment, which clarifies the authorising provision in relation to... This was a minimum period from contract to delivery with a view to ensuring the proper functioning of the internal market. As regards the 5th amendment, the definition of the fine was amended here and conditions were added to determine the circumstances relating to the amount of the penalty. The 6th amendment clarifies the characteristics of the local Agricultural Market Agency. The point here is that the director of the field branch of the agency responsible for the place of residence or residence of the buyer - that was the point - makes fines by means of an administrative decision. This is about the director of the Agricultural Market Agency. We're moving on to the last seven. This 7th amendment sets the date of entry into force. The law shall enter into force six months after the date of publication, except for Article. 41, which shall enter into force 30 days from the date of the announcement. As I said earlier, on 3 November 2016, the joint committees, i.e. the Committee on Agriculture and Rural Development and the Committee on Economic Affairs and Development, adopted the report of the committee, which was also provided to the State. So that the committees at the meeting on 3 November considered and adopted all the comments which were addressed by the subcommittee. Thank you very much. ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-15-sejm-30-1.u187	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! I want to present the opinion of the Law and Justice Club on this very important and long-awaited law, regulation. It responds mainly to the expectations of suppliers of agricultural raw materials, agricultural products and foodstuffs, because it also fell from this speaker many, many times - the position in the food chain, which we define: from field to table, the position of agricultural producers and producers of food products is the weakest. This chain would not have functioned if they had not produced products, whereas in the process of processing and trade their position is very often the weakest and they obtain a minimum, often non-covering cost of producing income. There are also - unfortunately, this is a phenomenon that has not been managed over the years of transformation - to remove entrepreneurs who are directly out of crime carrying out their interests. After all, we know companies - there are many in Poland - which bought products from suppliers, paying the first tranche, and then companies fell and it was not possible to enforce it under existing law. When farmers reported it to the authorities, reported it to the justice system, they often saw their hands folded: it's hard, they can't fix anything anymore, they're not able to enforce anything, companies with limited liability have such small capital, and there's no way to recover from the company's shareholders' assets. Therefore, this law, which provides a very real tool in the form of the Office for Competition and Consumer Protection, which can initiate proceedings both ex officio and as a result of a written request by traders, is a good solution. This applies primarily to large turnovers where the annual turnover in the year of the notification or in two consecutive years, in each of these years separately, exceeds 50,000. zł. This is not about - I apologize for colloquialism - those who trade small-scale parsley. These are important agricultural producers who could be in such situations. This applies to large companies, both commercial and customers, whose turnover exceeds PLN 100 million per year. The Act does not, of course, apply to members of cooperatives who are trading with their own cooperatives or to members of producer groups. It's natural, that's where it's regulated by other mechanisms. The Act also clarifies the use of the contractual advantage, in particular the unjustified termination of the contract or threat of termination of the contract, the assignment of only one party to the right to terminate the contract or to make the conclusion or continuation of the contract subject to other benefits. This is particularly noticeable in supermarket networks, where you try to force other benefits on suppliers, not related to this product. This law treats it as an unfair exploitation of the contractual advantage. The President of the UOKiK, as I have already mentioned, initiates proceedings of his own motion, which may also be preceded by an investigation. They have also been defined - this is a good indication of the legislator - the time limits when these proceedings must end. They can't last forever, they're precisely defined, and it has to end either by acknowledging that there was no unfair advantage, or by imposing penalties. Punishment is annoying, but it's supposed to be preventive. The imposition of a penalty on a company that is proven to be unfairly competitive, as well as on persons running the company, is a matter of the utmost importance. The aim is to eliminate the pathology in economic trade, eliminate the unfair exploitation of advantages, in which, as I mentioned at the beginning, above all food producers, with a weaker position, lose and all this affects their condition. That's why this bill is so expected. [[♪ The bell ♪]] Finally, I would also like to thank the subcommittee and its President, Mr Gołojuch, for the great work that has been done. This law deserves all its backing, and our club will support it. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-15-sejm-30-1.u191	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Kukiz Club15, I have the honour to present my position on the draft law against unfair exploitation of the advantage of contracts in the marketing of agricultural and food products, print No 790. The main objective of this bill is to eliminate unfair commercial practices between buyers and suppliers of agri-food products and between suppliers and buyers at each stage of the supply chain. It should be pointed out that the law is intended to work both ways. Today, the stronger side - large commercial networks, supermarkets - often use their advantage against the weaker ones, which are suppliers. The principle that it is the buyer who decides on prices, often below the cost of manufacturing of goods, has become common practice. Farmers often raise the problem of unfair contract terms, of forcing unfair cooperation rules by commercial networks, and the rules in force, whether it be the Civil Code, which lists in detail the prohibited contractual clauses, or the provisions of the Act on Combating Unfair Competition, or the Code of Good Practice, do not sufficiently secure the position of the supplier's farmer. The imbalance between the parties to the agreement is affected by the strength and size which large commercial networks are commonly used by the farmer. In this respect, the effects of such measures, namely the reduction in the quality of food products, the deterioration of the financial situation of suppliers and their investment potential, are not taken into account. Ultimately, the negative consequences of this type of practice are borne by unaware consumers, that is, all of us. It therefore appears that one of the main objectives of this project, namely to increase the powers of the President of the Office for Competition and Consumer Protection, which will be able, inter alia, to increase the powers of the President of the Office for Competition and Consumer Protection. impose a penalty on the supplier or the buyer, in particular a preventive function, i.e. By scaring away, they tend to obey the law. Such powers, in particular the right to impose severe penalties, are reasonable if they are subject to scrutiny. Supervision is important for several reasons, but the most important is ensuring regularity and eliminating discretion in imposing penalties. The second important element is the severity of sanctions. Only the penalty that the punished will suffer will have the desired effect, i.e. prevent similar infringements in the future. Therefore, in my opinion, it is only up to 3% of the amount of the fine. the turnover achieved in the financial year preceding the year of application of the penalty should be clarified by introducing a range from - to so that the penalty is sure and unavoidable. I think that the greatest benefit of adopting this Act will be to eliminate the feeling of harm in farmers, because their position in the supply chain of agricultural and food products is very weak. Unfortunately, they are often abused and deceived because of this. In general, contractors do not treat farmers as equal partners, purchases lower agricultural prices, dairy and slaughterhouses do not care about ensuring a good price for their suppliers, because they import products from abroad at lower prices, and plant protection products and fertilisers vendors raise prices without any reasonable justification. Recently, farmers are also complaining that the tax authorities are asking them to return VAT when the company that sold the fuel to the farmer has fallen or simply ceased its activities in unexplained circumstances. Thus, even the authorities of a country unscrupulously exploit the weakness of the position of farmers. Therefore, I believe that this law should be an incentive to align the status of farmers with the status of other operators in the supply chain and to encourage further action in this direction. In view of the above, I would like to present to the Marshal an amendment to this law. Kukiz,15 will vote in favour of this law depending on the examination of these amendments. Thank you very much.

So it will only concern a narrow, very narrow group of farmers, because most of the farmers' operations are carried out with many partners, run through a network of small buying points or small establishments. So at least because of that, she's absolutely not going to be useful. Secondly, this second partner, the potentially stronger partner, the one, as we say today, who uses the advantage of a contract or uses unfair practices on the market, will have to have a turnover of more than PLN 100 million. So how many such partners do farmers today have, with which they trade directly and whose turnover exceeds 100 million PLN? It's not a buying-in point, it's not a small processing plant, it's huge entities, that's, let's say, super- and hypermarket networks to which this law essentially narrows down. And this is the first point that this law will absolutely not apply to Polish farmers, it will concern only a few entities that operate in the field of food trade. In this respect, the interest of the state budget is secured, because penalties from such entities can be quite large on a macro scale and they will fund the state budget. ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-15-sejm-30-1.u202	Thank you. Mr Ireneusz Gaina, the Free and Solidarnia Members' Circle, will now speak.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-15-sejm-30-1.u203	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Freedom and Solidarnia Circle, I present my position on the Government's draft law against unfair exploitation of contract advantages in the marketing of agricultural and food products, printing No. 790, and the report of the Committee on Economic Affairs and Development and the Committee on Agriculture and Rural Development, print No. 983. The purpose of the proposed law is to eliminate in practice the supply of agricultural raw materials and food from unfair commercial practices, and this is the objective of the most appropriate. The pressure to obtain the lowest price from the buyer can actually affect the quality of the food products offered to the customer. Agricultural producers and processors of agricultural products, as a result of the pressure exerted by operators with a dominant position on the market, are often forced to replace in the production process good quality raw materials with worse ones. This is as harmful as possible to consumers. There are very many examples of such practices: imposing designs of contracts without the possibility of negotiating them, one-sided termination of contracts, forcing the level of purchase prices, unjustified extension of payment deadlines for the goods delivered and other completely not having anything to do with the essential subject-matter of the trade. From a formal point of view, suppliers have the right to pursue their claims under applicable law. This may take place before courts in private legal proceedings. Unfortunately, this entails considerable costs, and the proceedings themselves are long-term. Entities who attempt such action shall be punished by the recipients by the termination of the contract, which only aggravates their position. Wholesalers of agricultural products often operate from force, using shelves, pallets, for space in the refrigerator, for exposure, for additional costs of newspapers, etc. This is not fiction, this phenomenon must be countered. On the other hand, the administrative procedure for resolving disputes through administrative proceedings conducted by the President of the Office for Competition and Consumer Protection should significantly relieve suppliers in a weaker position in the investigation of their claims and be an incentive to stop unfair practices by stronger consumers. The introduction of financial administrative penalties for unfair use of the contractual advantage should result in the cessation of such practices. The problem of unfair practices in trade with network users does not exist only in Poland. The different countries of the European Union have taken various measures to eliminate these pathologies. There are no EU rules imposing the use of selected solutions, and we can therefore try to solve this problem ourselves. I hope that the regulations proposed in the Act will meet the expectations of it. The use by large counterparties of terms and conditions that result in the transfer of economic risk to agricultural producers in order to maximise profits often takes place at the border of law. This practice poses a major threat to the whole food industry. It is fundamental that the supply of agri-food products should be based on clear and clear principles that shape good relations between its links and apply equally to all market participants regardless of economic potential. There is no doubt that the draft law submitted is necessary, but there is some doubt in its form that the Public Prosecutor General of the State Treasury pointed out in its opinion of 29 August 2016. Above all, we are talking about the wording of the provisions of the Article. Paragraph 7 2 and 3, which require clarification not to duplicate the provisions of the Act on Combating Unfair Competition and the Act on Competition and Consumer Protection and do not create a legal mess. It should also be settled whether the content of the article. 30 projects after the entry into force of the Act will allow businesses to formulate claims for damages where, after disclosure of their data on the website of the Office for Competition and Consumer Protection as those who apply unfair practices, a final decision will then be repealed against them declaring the act they have committed to be an unfair use of the advantage of the contract. Unfortunately, during the drafting of the bill in committees, these doubts were not eliminated. A law against unfair practices in the marketing of agricultural and food products should be adopted, but we believe that its current form needs to be improved. I hope that at the third reading or in the work of the Senate the bill will be refined. About Free and Solidarni will support the bill. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

These comments were received from the Conference of Financial Companies in Poland, from the Confederation of Leviatan, from the Board of Directors of the Funds and Assets, as well as from the National Bank of Poland and the Financial Supervision Commission. Interested parties participated in multi-hour discussions during meetings of the Committee on Public Finances. The solutions proposed in the project aroused a lot of controversy, raised opposition, and the analysis of solutions indicated that the taxation of investment funds would have a very negative impact on finance in general, even the Polish capital market. As a result of obvious substantive arguments, the applicants submitted a self-correction which withdraws many of these adverse solutions. The amendment was accepted by the members of the committee and will, of course, result in significantly lower tax revenues for investment funds. And the remaining provisions left behind in the project are such that investors of the funds will withdraw from the Polish capital market. The project also includes a number of legislative and editorial changes, and the following article. 3 relating to the Tax Order contains even a procedural error, because it contains proposals for solutions which were not the subject of the first reading of the project, and thus were not included as issues in the original project at all. The first part of the project remains unchanged. By the votes of the PiS Members, the project leaves the tax-free amount unchanged.
