In today's speech more widely than in the previous session of the Sejm, I would also like to refer to the part of the draft law on the revision of the rules on taxation of investment funds, i.e. to amendments to the Corporate Income Tax Act. In the view of the applicants, the current use of closed investment funds often boils down to tax optimisation by large taxpayers, which results in a preference for small and medium-sized enterprises. As we can see in the explanatory memorandum, this situation has a negative impact on the competitiveness of smaller players, weakening their market position. The question is whether the proposed changes will not result in a bathing child being fired, undermining citizens' confidence in investment, investment, which the promotion of which among Poles is consistent with the plan for responsible development. It is worth mentioning a few details, which were indicated in a letter sent to clubs and parliamentary circles by the Board of Directors of Funds and Assets, a voluntary organization of associations of investment funds operating on the Polish market. In the opinion of the authorities of the Chambers, the law will achieve objectives contrary to those assumed. As I have said, the aim of the project developers is to eliminate the use of the investment fund as tax optimisation, while the project provides for taxation of all closed investment funds, as well as specialised open investment funds applying the investment policy of the closed fund. The House stresses that funds closed to a large extent are funds offered to a wide range of investors, often small ones, whose purpose of investment is absolutely not optimization. It therefore considers that if the purpose of the regulation is, as explained by the justification, to deprive tax efficiency of structures involving closed investment funds and tax transparent entities, it would suffice to make an exception to investments in transparent entities in an entity exempting income tax funds. As a result, these structures would be taxed as would other taxpayers, while the remaining investment activities would continue to be promoted. In addition, the representatives of the Chamber indicate that the proposed changes will result in the fact that, unlike shareholders of capital companies, participants in closed investment funds will not be able to benefit from the income tax exemption in respect of the dividends received, which makes these funds completely ineffective as investment instruments. It is also important to raise the issue of a drastically short period of vacathio legis, which, with the long-term functioning of the funds, can adversely affect investment activities, and it is worth remembering that investment fund participants are a large group - about 2 million people. I ask the applicants to comment on the comments submitted by the Board of Directors of the Funds and Assets, with particular regard to those which I have quoted. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-15-sejm-30-1.u277	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour on behalf of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice to present my position on the draft Law No 897 on the amendment of the Animal Protection Act. This has become necessary in the project - the rules have been detailed and extended in order to regulate more comprehensively the policy of the protection and prevention of the homelessness of animals and to carry out the tasks of self-governments in this area. Overpopulation of pets and scale of homelessness according to the NIK report, which appeared this year, have several reasons, of which the most important are the low awareness and lack of education in this matter among Poles, with particular attention to the issue of sterilization and castration as the main method of preventing uncontrolled reproduction, as well as the lack of legal opportunities according to local authorities, which cannot spend money on these activities, especially as regards animals that have their owners. According to the report, no one in the municipality is still able to effectively prevent the homelessness of animals. Most of them did not implement a labelling plan for all animals or put in place real incentives in terms of castration or sterilization of dogs and cats with owners. Some municipalities have not even ensured mandatory sterilization by hostels. As a result, the number of animals caught is increasing, as well as the expenditure incurred in caring for them. Although more and more homeless animals find their new home, there are still far fewer of them than those that go to shelters. The results of the checks indicate that the way to really solve the problem of animal homeless, including the reduction of the costs necessary to care for animals, is not to put animals in better and more expensive shelters, but, above all, to programme and finance preventive measures that, in the long term, eliminate or marginalise the phenomenon of animal homelessness. One of the follow-up requests is to allow the Animal Protection Act to provide financing for animals from municipalities for the sterilization of animals outside shelters, while respecting the rights of their owners. The municipalities did not direct any incentive to animal owners to castrate and sterilize them. They explained that, in the current legal state, funding for these procedures is not acceptable. Many municipalities wanted to introduce these measures in their programme concerning the care of homeless animals in the framework of resolutions, but unfortunately the voivodships claimed that there was no statutory authorisation or legal basis for these actions to be considered lawful. Also in the proceedings, some courts agreed to this position, i.e. the position of the voivods. Therefore, among others. in this parliamentary project, to the catalogue contained in Article. 11 The Animal Protection Act, which is a closed catalogue, introduces one provision on the possibility for local governments to sterilize their own animals. In the perspective of a few years, all municipalities will benefit from this, because it is the animals that own the largest number of reproductions. This is a positive and necessary change to ensure better protection of animals. The sterilization could be managed on a voluntary basis within the programme, and if the municipalities have their resources, there is no reason not to introduce this provision and not to allow them to do so. We therefore recommend that the High House adopt this draft law also in line with the position of the Committee on Agriculture and Local Government. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-15-sejm-30-1.u281	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Kukiz Club, 15 I have the honour to present my position on the draft law amending the Animal Protection Act, print No 897. The main objective of the draft amendment to this law is the need to regulate the rights of homeless animals which, unfortunately, in the past have been treated as municipal waste in an unworthy manner. The current shape of the law did not give municipalities the possibility to comprehensively prevent the homelessness of animals. Following the adoption of the proposed amendment, preventing the homelessness of animals and providing care for homeless animals will be the responsibility of the municipalities themselves. This will oblige them to adopt and finance their own resources programmes including, inter alia,: providing homeless animals with shelters, caring for free living cats, including feeding them, catching homeless animals, searching for owners for homeless animals. In addition, the Act will facilitate sterilization, with full respect for the rights of owners or other persons under whom animals remain protected, which can clearly lead to the elimination of the problem of homelessness in the long term. In view of the above, Kukiz15 will vote in favour of the bill amending the Animal Protection Act. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-15-sejm-30-1.u283	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! I have the honour to present the position of the Poselski Club on the proposal for a parliamentary bill amending the Animal Protection Act. [[The lynx bill.]] In the framework of the amendment of the Animal Protection Act in Art. Article 11 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 Another, i.e. prevention of the homelessness of animals, was added to the tasks of the municipalities themselves, which are to provide care and capture for homeless animals. The provision on the care programme for homeless animals and the prevention of homelessness of animals has been added that it may include a plan for their sterilization, castration, while respecting the rights of animal owners. According to the Supreme Audit Chamber, the previous experience of municipalities that have decided to implement such programmes is to confirm that sterilization and castration are investments that not only improve animal welfare but also save public money. Pre-emptive actions programmed and funded by local authorities have the best effect. The current law limits the catalogue of activities of local governments. Ununiform practice of municipalities causes that only some of them encourage owners of dogs and other animals to sterilize and castrate through the funding of such treatments. The introduction of the law does not oblige changes to current programmes. In practice, this provision may not change much. It may be that the municipalities which have so far engaged in financing such operations will continue to do so, and those who have done nothing in this direction will not change this. Legislative changes lack provisions on the central register of pet owners and the obligation to label them. Such a procedure would allow quick identification of the owners of the animals found. Thus, there would not be so many cases of abandoning animals without any consequences. The lack of an effective identification system causes many dogs and cats to disappear after being caught by their animals. They are often killed or sent to shelters, which generates costs, and it is difficult to hold the owners of these animals responsible. By the way, I would like to point out that Poland has not yet signed the European Convention for the Protection of Accompanying Animals adopted by the Council of Europe on 13 November 1987. The Convention requires that the reproduction of pet animals for trade is permitted only under the supervision of the relevant State authority. It's written in the article. 8. State action towards total inhibition of shady farming is very important not only in terms of the increasing costs of municipalities, but also of the associated pathologies causing suffering and homelessness of dogs and cats. Although this project does not solve many problems, it is certainly a step forward and needed. Sterilization is one of the cheapest ways to fight animal homeless. The officials of many municipalities do not take these measures because they believe that, in their current legal state, the financing of sterilization and castration procedures is not acceptable. The revision of the rules will change this state of affairs. I am a member of a team of animal friends and I wonder, I have a question for Members writing this bill: Why is this such a narrow scope? Because we worked in a team on a very wide range of amendments to the Animal Protection Act. This was replaced by the term "minister responsible for home affairs'. First of all, it was about individuals such as schools, school or other libraries, as well as universities. First of all, it was about educational units. The aim of this amendment is to make use of EU funds instead of the budget resources of the Ministry. The next amendment concerns Article. 183 Telecommunication Law and aims to amend part of the provisions on telecommunications charges. Currently, all fee issues are regulated in a single regulation, which must simply be revised annually. The last amendment concerns provisions on support for the development of telecommunications services and networks. Indicates the rules to be applied to access to infrastructure or telecommunications network accordingly. The amendment also states that the relevant provisions of Section I of Chapter 3 of the Telecommunication Law of 16 July 2004 apply to the decision on access to technical infrastructure. The last amendment was the introduction of an additional article. 23a, which is the consequence of this article, which I was talking about at the beginning about the amendments related to the Ministry of Digitalization. The aim of this amendment is to maintain the Minister of Infrastructure's current regulation on the amount, timing and method of payment of the annual telecommunications fee. Ladies and gentlemen! [[Adjournment from 10 a.m. 17 minutes 04 hours 17 min 11]] I'm reopening the session. We proceed with the examination of item 13. of the agenda: First reading of the government draft law amending the Act on Trading in Financial Instruments and some other laws (print No 991). I ask the Undersecretary of State at the Ministry of Finance of Mr Leszek Skiba to present the justification for the bill.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-15-sejm-30-1.u341	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The bill submitted for amending the Act on Trading in Financial Instruments and some other laws responds to requests made for a long time by capital market participants in Poland, as well as the national supervisory authority of this market. The project introduces three fundamental changes to the functioning of the capital market in Poland. Firstly, it is the abolition of the division of the regulated market into the stock market and the OTC market and the inclusion of these two types of market in the same concept of the regulated market. In recent years, the changes in the rules have actually brought about an end to the differences in the functioning of the stock market and the over-the-counter market. Currently, the OTC market as regards the rights and obligations of issuers, including in particular information obligations, reporting or categories of entities that may be members of those markets, as well as transactions that may be concluded in those markets, is no different from the stock market that is not officially quoted. Thus, by abolishing the division of the regulated market into the stock market and the OTC market, the formalistic non-justifiable legal division of the market will be eliminated both at the level of European law and in other Member States. Secondly, this is the introduction of a derivatives account institution. The Act of 5 December 2014 amending the Act on Trading in Financial Instruments and certain other laws abolished the requirement to register securities admitted to organised trading in the securities deposit system. However, this has led to a regulatory gap in the recording of derivatives, which makes it difficult for the Financial Supervision Commission to have access to information on all admitted to trading of organised derivatives and the activities of individual investors in the trading of those financial instruments. The proposed new derivative account institution closes this legal gap. Thirdly, it is a change of the authority issuing the authorisation to conduct a regulated market from the Minister of Development and Finance to the Financial Supervision Commission. Pursuant to the Act on Trading in Financial Instruments currently in force, the operation of the stock exchange shall be authorised by the Minister responsible for Financial Institutions at the request of the person concerned, as assessed by the Commission for Financial Supervision. This solution makes it possible, as a general rule, to carry out double operations and, consequently, to unduly extend the process of recognising an application for authorisation to hold a stock exchange. The solution proposed by this draft law means shortening the licensing process in Poland of stock exchanges and focusing matters related to the supervision of the capital market in one institution. The bill submitted to the High House was the subject of extensive consultation with the capital market environment. It was positively assessed by the supervisory authority, the Commission for Financial Supervision. High Izbo, I request the support of the bill submitted. On behalf of the government, I would like to express my conviction that its adoption will contribute to improving the functioning of the capital market. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-15-sejm-30-1.u350	Thank you. The discussion is closed.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-15-sejm-30-1.u351	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club, I present my position on the government draft law amending the Act on Trading in Financial Instruments and some other laws. In the explanatory memorandum to the draft law, we read that according to the article. 15 of the Act of 29 July 2005 on Trading in Financial Instruments The regulated market in the territory of the Republic of Poland includes the stock market and the OTC market. Over-the-counter market of a less formal nature imposed minor requirements on issuers seeking admission to trading of their securities. The programme was intended to be a market for small and medium-sized companies with growth potential which did not meet the requirements of the stock market. In fact, today the OTC market is no different from the stock market, which is not an official listing market. There are no differences between the stock market and the OTC market in the rights and obligations of issuers, including in particular information obligations, pre- and post-trade reporting or categories of entities that may be members of those markets, as well as in transactions that may be included in those markets. Thus, in the current legal situation, the most important differences between the sub-types of the regulated market are: - another entity issuing a market authorisation (Minister of Finance for the listed market, Financial Supervision Commission for the OTC market), - other capital requirements of the stock exchange companies and the OTC market (PLN 10 million and PLN 5 million respectively), - another legal basis defining the type, scope and form of current and periodic information published by issuers of securities. In summary, the differences between the stock market and the OTC market are not significant enough to justify the distinction between two sub-types of the regulated market. It is therefore reasonable to abolish the division of the regulated market into the stock market and over-the-counter market. The changes made over the last several years to the rules on the functioning of the national capital market, notably the Act on Trading in Financial Instruments, have led to a levelling of differences in the functioning of the stock market and over-the-counter market. The provisions of Union law also do not provide for such a distinction between operators of regulated markets, in particular Directive 2004/39/EC of the European Parliament and of the Council of 21 April 2004 on markets in financial instruments amending Council Directives 85/611/EEC and 93/6/EEC and Directive 2000/12/EC of the European Parliament and of the Council and repealing Council Directive 93/22/EEC. Urz. EU L 145, 30.04.2004, p. 1, go on: MiFID). Furthermore, the definition of a regulated market as an OTC market may mislead about his character. The term "over-the-counter market' is not reflected in the commonly functioning nomenclature resulting from European rules transposed by Member States into national legal systems. The aim of the project, as the applicants present, is to abolish the division of the regulated market into the stock market and over-the-counter market. As a result, these two types of market would be covered by the concept of a regulated market. Therefore, the project assumes that the company currently operating the stock exchange market, i.e. the Warsaw Stock Exchange SA, and the company operating the over-the-counter market, i.e. BondSpot SA, will become companies operating two separate regulated markets. The draft provides for appropriate transitional periods for the adjustment of both companies' activities. As a result of the proposed amendments, the provisions of other laws will be adapted to the amended provisions of the Act, the Accounting Act, the Act on toll motorways and the National Road Fund, the Act on Energy Law, the Act on Commercial Companies Code, the Act on Trade Exchanges, the Act on Insolvency, the Act on Investment Funds and the Management of Alternative Investment Funds, the Act on Public Offering and the Conditions of Introduction of Financial Instruments to Organized Trading, and on Public Companies, as well as the Act on Supervision of the Financial Market. In view of the need to replace the terms "markets" and "markets" by means of "markets" it will also be necessary to amend a number of implementing acts. In addition, the project envisages the introduction of rules on the institution's derivatives account. As provided for by the Act of 5 December 2014 amending the Act on Trading in Financial Instruments and certain other laws, the abolition of the requirement to register securities of derivatives admitted to organised trading results in the need to regulate the rules for recording derivatives in order to ensure the safety of trading and effective supervision of the forward market. Regulation introduced e.g. define the entities authorised to keep records of derivatives, specify the scope of the authorisations to keep such records while identifying the recording facilities on which derivatives are to be recorded, and specify the rules for determining the holders of the derivative, the rules for transferring rights and obligations from certain derivatives. The consequences of the proposed changes are also the need to equip the supervisory authority with effective supervisory tools to ensure effective supervision of the safety and regularity of secondary trading of financial instruments, in particular to determine the scope of investments made by entities not operating in the territory of the Republic of Poland, which are parties to stock exchange transactions and which are not obliged under the regulations in force in Poland to record transactions concluded on derivatives on any accounts. To this end, the draft law provides for the power of the Financial Supervision Commission to apply directly to these entities for relevant information. At the same time, it is necessary to stress that the changes envisaged in this respect do not affect the scope of supervision of investment firms at European level in the field of capital market law. Therefore, supervision of the entities referred to in Article. Article 31 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 point 2 of the Act, i.e. investment firms that are not members of the stock exchange which are not engaged in brokerage activities in the territory of the Republic of Poland, will continue to be held by the competent supervisory authorities of the State of its head office. The consequences of the proposed changes will also be to adapt the provisions of other laws, i.e. the Settlement Finality Act in payment and securities settlement systems and the rules for the supervision of these systems and bankruptcy laws. In addition, it will be necessary, in particular, to amend the Finance Minister's Regulation of 24 September 2012 on the modalities and conditions of conduct of investment firms, banks referred to in Article. OJ L 347, 20.12.2013, p. 671. 2 of the Act on Trading in Financial Instruments, and Trust Banks, as well as the issuing by the Minister responsible for Financial Institutions of a new Regulation pursuant to Article. Article 94 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 point 5 of the Act. The draft law also provides for a change of the authority issuing the permit to hold the stock exchange. According to Art. The Commission shall adopt implementing acts laying down detailed rules for the implementation of this Regulation in accordance with Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council laying down detailed rules for the implementation of this Regulation. 1 and 2 of the current Act on Trading in Financial Instruments requires authorisation by the Minister responsible for Financial Institutions at the request of the person concerned, as assessed by the Financial Supervision Commission. The application shall be submitted through the Financial Supervision Commission, which shall assess the application formally (where formal deficiencies are found, the committee shall decide to leave the application without recognition) and shall then give its opinion. Only after these operations have been carried out, shall the request, together with the opinion, be forwarded by the committee of the Minister responsible for financial institutions. Such a solution makes it possible to double the same activities and, as a consequence, extend the time limit for the recognition of an application for authorisation to hold a stock exchange. Therefore, the essential purpose of changing the authority issuing the authorisation to conduct a regulated market from the Minister responsible for Financial Institutions to the Financial Supervision Commission is to shorten the licensing process in Poland of the exchanges of financial instruments and to focus matters related to the supervision of the capital market in full in the statutoryly appointed body, the Financial Supervision Commission. The Commission for Financial Supervision would at the same time be the competent authority for the withdrawal of such authorisation and for the prohibition of a regulated market. The project assumes that the existing provisions will apply to proceedings initiated and not completed. In that case, the authorisation decision will be adopted on the basis of the amended wording as a new Article. 25 of the Act, however, by the same body before which proceedings were initiated, which should ensure the smooth conduct of the proceedings initiated and not completed before the date of entry into force of the proposed law. The draft law is not contrary to European Union law. Euros. The Bank's balance sheet at the end of 2015 amounted to 349 million and the Bank's profit in 2015 amounted to 3 million euro. Currently, the bank is developing its lending on a wider scale. Continuing with the theme of the International Bank for Economic Cooperation, it must be said that this is another project which concerns the consent of the President of the Republic of Poland to ratify the Protocol amending the Agreement on the establishment and operation of the International Bank for Economic Cooperation. On 18 December 1990, the Bank Council decided to replace ECU's transfer rubles in the bank's accounts, change the name of the multilateral settlement agreement, change some of the bank's business objectives and decision-making issues with Germany's withdrawal from the bank. On 25 November 2014, a protocol was drawn up on the arrangement of technical issues related to the withdrawal from Cuba and Hungary, the introduction into the Statute of the euro Bank in place of ECU, changes to certain Member States or their names, and the participation of Member States. As a result of these changes, Poland increased its capital paid out from EUR 15.5 million to EUR 22.45 million, which resulted from a proportional split between the countries that occurred from the bank. The Polish People's Party welcomes the functioning of the International Bank of Economic Cooperation and fully supports the ratification of the protocols. According to a study of the public opinion, as much as 60% Poles surveyed believe that we should conduct business and trade with the Russian Federation. Given also the changing socio-economic situation in the world, the Polish People's Party considers that there is a need to resume economic cooperation with the Russian Federation to a large extent, and a functioning international bank based in Moscow will certainly be an institution to help develop economic relations. Thank you very much.

The draft presented to the Sejm concerns changes in the protocols of 18 December 1990 and 25 November 2014, which relate mainly to the ordering of a number of technical issues and the attempt to set new objectives of the bank's activities. As far as technical issues are concerned, they must be arranged in such a way that the bank can operate efficiently. On the other hand, with regard to the bank's strategy, we maintain our sceptical position on the draft ratification of the protocol drawn up in Moscow on 18 December 1990, printing 875. In the context of creating a new economic policy on the Eastern direction, as expressed by the recent visit of Deputy Prime Minister Mateusz Morawiecki in Belarus, it should be considered whether there will be new tasks under this policy in which the International Bank of Economic Cooperation could participate. This kind of action in the eastern direction is part of political pragmatism and brings tangible benefits to Polish entrepreneurs and Polish citizens both now and in the long term. We are therefore looking for flexibility and dexterity in the formulation of the new strategy of the International Bank of Economic Cooperation. At the same time, attention should be paid to the significant reorientation of the policy towards our western neighbours, who are increasingly publicising the view that they do not intend to bear further costs of the conflict in Ukraine. This creates such a geopolitical situation that Poland has a very small margin for finding favorable solutions for itself. Perhaps the bank in question creates opportunities for cooperation in the eastern directions. This should be checked through pilot projects. With regard to the draft Act on ratification of the Protocol amending the Agreement on the Establishment and Activity of the International Bank of Economic Cooperation, drawn up in Warsaw on 25 November 2014, the Free and Solidarni Circle supports the submitted project, recognising that it is intended to formally regulate the current business of the bank. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-15-sejm-30-1.u389	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! The honourable Mrs Agata Borowiec has put forward a very interesting question: How did the protocol of 25 November 2014 come to be signed and is Poland obliged to ratify this protocol? I kindly explain that the case started much earlier, because this concerns the creation of an international bank, while the history of the signing of the protocol starts in principle on 5 December 2014, because the protocol was drawn up much earlier, maybe not so much, on 25 November 2014 in Moscow, but on 5 December 2014, the Council of Ministers approved the signing of the Protocol amending the Agreement on the establishment and operation of the International Bank of Economic Cooperation. Therefore, on 30 March 2015, a protocol drawn up several months earlier, i.e. on 25 November 2014 in Moscow, was signed. On 18 August 2016, the Minister of Finance submitted a request for ratification of those protocols. And the second question was whether Poland is obliged to ratify these protocols. There is no such compulsion, but it is beneficial for us, we must go inside, create a possible procedure to get out of this so that we do not suffer losses. Today it would be advisable, it is in the interest of Poland to ratify this protocol. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-15-sejm-30-1.u399	Dear Marshal, Minister! High Izbo! I am pleased to present the position of the Poselski Club on the government draft law amending certain laws in order to facilitate the sale of food by farmers. The changes in agriculture in the 1990s and the entry into force of European Union legislation in 2004 created a new reality in the food trade. Small operators processing agricultural raw materials have disappeared from the legal market. Of course, processing in farms is still functioning, but in the grey zone. For many years, attempts have been made to resolve this situation, resulting in regulations that enable small - limited - local enterprises to be created, as well as direct sales, from farms, of products processed by non-industrial methods. In practice, despite the demand of consumers for such processed food, this processing branch is not developing at the pace to which market potential would indicate. The reason for this is the legal constraints which are not compatible with the actual market situation of such products. There is still a belief that food from small manufacturers is a cheap alternative, and in practice these products have become quality food and non-cheap food. New forms of distribution have been created, such as co-operatives, local health food and organic food stores. Therefore, in order to develop this part of the agricultural market including food produced in small farms producing traditional and ecological methods, legal changes are necessary. But changes must be thoughtful, adapted to changing situations. It is about changes that would really safeguard consumer rights, but also, more broadly, allow farmers to operate. There is a need to create a development ladder from mini-processing, through small processing to businesses. When discussing the draft law amending certain laws to facilitate the sale of food by farmers, it is worth considering the fact that, under the rules set out by the EU direct sales regulations, Member States have more freedom than is currently presented in Polish law. Many EU countries, such as France and Italy, do not treat the catalogue of products as a closed catalogue, but do not stop by the fact that European Union law lists all elements of this catalogue that there are no other options than those mentioned. These countries are expanding the list of products sold as part of direct sales. In France you can sell such products as milk, eggs, fruit, vegetables, bread, foie gras, yogurt, cheese, ready-to-eat dishes. [[You can't foie gras.]] In Italy, however, direct sales were extended to include wine and other products of the wine sector, fruit and vegetable products, olive products, including olive oil. Polish regulations lack the same extension, resulting in considerable restrictions on the sale of a wide range of processed products such as juices, jams, purée, home bread, raw meat and some of its products, oils, butter, cheese, milk and many others. In Poland, the legal sale of processed products involves specific high requirements, which is why this is mainly done by specialised processing plants, which requires considerable financial expenditure. Small food producers are unable to afford such production and therefore do not participate in the market for these goods. This results in limited access to the best quality naturally produced processed products and the functioning of the grey zone in this production department. In many EU countries, regulations have been developed to allow the sale of natural products from small and medium-sized farms, while maintaining the hygiene and quality standards of the food sold. The production of small quantities of food products and therefore the associated low revenues do not allow the investments required for their safe processing, so the aim is to allow more than one farmer to use the production base. And an amendment like that would be useful here. Now - I'm not sure because you've tabled an amendment, but I don't know what it says, so maybe I'm saying it again. On the other hand, we could use such an amendment so that there is a chance to use business incubators - not only do we notice it, but also many clubs - and within this programme we could build a base for small processing. This is a model example of incubatory entrepreneurship. Farmers, without investing, would have the opportunity to check whether the processing they want to undertake will work in the business model. I think there's a lot of other improvements I could use. Today, when I observe work in committee and subcommittee, I see so many downsides that the Modern Posel Club, without yet knowing these amendments of the state, cannot support this project, although, as you know, farmers are waiting for it and consumers are waiting for it. [[♪ The bell ♪]] Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-15-sejm-30-1.u423	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Currently, there is no uniform approach in the territory of the Republic of Poland regarding the competence of the authorities authorised to register, the approval of retail establishments, the proverbial direct sales in which production is carried out, including the processing of food, i.e. production of meats, sausages, fruit juices, vegetables or pierogies. At the same time, the current national tax rules lay down different rules on the taxation of farmers' sales of products processed from their own cultivation, farming or farming, without indicating clearly when such sales are exempt from income tax. In particular, the proposed law plans to define the concept of agricultural retail trade, namely the retail trade consisting of selling to the final consumer, without the participation of the intermediary, food derived wholly or partly from its own cultivation, farming, farming of food business operators. The second thing, very important and important, is the identification of authorities competent to supervise the safety of food produced in the framework of agricultural retail trade. Here, the supervision of the appropriate health quality of products of animal origin or food containing food of non-animal origin undergoes veterinary inspection, while the supervision of the relevant health quality of food of non-animal origin remains the responsibility of the authorities of the State Sanitary Inspection. Another important thing is to grant the Minister responsible for agriculture, in agreement with the Minister responsible for health, the competence to define, by means of regulations, limits on the size of activities carried out for one entity in the framework of agricultural retail trade. Here I will immediately reply to Mrs Borowiec that the draft regulation on the maximum quantity of food sold in the framework of agricultural retail trade and the scope and method of documenting it is already prepared and will enter into force on the date of the entry into force of the law, namely on 1 January 2017. It is also a matter of competence to lay down hygiene requirements for operators engaged in agricultural retail trade in products of animal origin and food submitted within the scope of possible adaptations of those requirements to the requirements set out in Annex II to the Regulation. Mr President, Madam President, the draft regulation is already being prepared and will also enter into force with the entry into force of the law, which is 1 January 2017. It is also very important to introduce tax preferences for agricultural producers engaged in retail trade, taking into account inter alia. cases where the slaughter of animals in order to obtain meat for production in accordance with Union law must be carried out in an approved slaughterhouse veterinarian and the production of flour, groats or bran, juices, oil from its own cultivation takes place in specialised establishments. In this respect, it is proposed in particular to exempt from tax income tax on natural persons income from agricultural retail trade up to 20 000. per year. ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-15-sejm-30-1.u489	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The proposed amendments aim first and foremost to ensure that actions under the Rural Development Programme 2014-2020 are implemented more rapidly and correctly, including simplifications for beneficiaries of individual support instruments. In order to achieve these objectives, it is also necessary to amend the Act on the national producer registration system, the farm records and the records of payment applications and the Act on payments under direct support schemes. And no one deserves such help more than just a Polish farmer who often struggles with the hardships of everyday life on a farm that no ordinary man could handle. As Kukiz,15 we are in favour of this law because in our opinion it is necessary to do everything in our power to show respect for the Polish farmer and try to give him a decent life. And you can do it just like this. by simplifying certain specific procedures, which will lead to faster implementation of solutions within the framework of the 2014-2020 RDPs. However, with the approval of this draft law by Kukiz.15, certain worrying provisions, which are included in this document, cannot be overlooked. The explanatory memorandum states that, at the request of the Agency for Restructuring and Modernisation of Agriculture, the provisions concerning the implementing entity referred to as the "Transfer of knowledge and information activities' and "Advisory services, farm management services and substitution services' have been amended. Moreover, the implementation of these tasks has not been entrusted to anyone other than ARiMR. The selection of the entity responsible for the implementation of these projects has been waived under the public procurement rules. The reason for this change has been identified among others. the fact that such a choice would be a complex and long-term process, which would inevitably lead to delays in the implementation of these projects. In addition, it was noted that the selection by tender might nevertheless not lead to the correct implementation of the programme assumptions. No one states that choosing ARiMR as a contractor must be a bad move, but it is certainly just an ad hoc method that does not solve this problem in a long-term way. Perhaps instead of improvising in this respect, it would be better to simplify procedures for public procurement so that similar situations like this are not duplicated? Finally, one more questionable aspect of the bill is worth mentioning. If ARiMR first recommended himself as an implementing entity for certain activities in the framework of the 2014-2020 RDPs, arguing that it has adequate staff resources and organisational structure, why is it further empowered to appoint specialists in the implementation of cases outside of the implementing entity's body, since ArimR is supposed to be well prepared for this? Despite these doubts, we are in favour of this law because it will help Polish farmers. I recommend that Kukiz15 vote in favour of this law. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-15-sejm-30-1.u513	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I have the honour to present to the High House the report of the National Defence Committee on the government draft law amending the law on the universal obligation to defend the Republic of Poland and some other laws, print No. 966. The Sejm at its meeting on 3 November in accordance with Article. 39 (1) (a) 2 of the Rules of Procedure submitted a draft law amending the Act on the universal obligation to defend the Republic of Poland and certain other laws to the Committee on National Defence for consideration. Let us remind you that the Act provides for the creation of a fifth type of Armed Forces of the Republic of Poland, the Territorial Defence Army, and the appointment of a commander to carry out his duties with the help of the Military Command of Territorial Defence. The most important reason for establishing the Territorial Defence Army is the change in the geopolitical and strategic situation of Poland. The attack of the Russian Federation on Ukraine is a strong proof that the situation in our part of the world is not as stable as we have assumed until recently, and consequently the modernization and development of the Armed Forces of the Republic of Poland is particularly important and urgent. The creation of the Territorial Defence Army gives the opportunity to substantially increase in a short time the Armed Forces of the Republic of Poland with troops of over 50,000. soldiers, soldiers, which should be emphasized, not only well trained and equipped, but also characterized by high idealism and determination to take up service. Therefore, speaking of the amended law, it should be said that today we are talking about a document which is fundamentally important from the point of view of the security of the Polish state. The National Defence Commission was sitting yesterday, i.e. November 14th. Eight legislative amendments were tabled during the meeting, which were accepted and included in the report by the Committee on National Defence, print No 1008. Five substantive amendments were tabled, two of which were accepted by the committee and included in the report. The first concerned the approval of further participation of a soldier in non-governmental organisations, and the second concerned the possibility of transferring an OT soldier only within the Territorial Defence Army. Three amendments were rejected. One of them was reported as a minority request. The Committee on National Defence after considering this project at its meeting on 14 November this year. asks the High Sejm to approve the attached bill. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

Due to the deteriorating situation in our region of Europe, Poland faced a dilemma in how to increase the state's defence potential in a relatively short period of time. The fact that Poland needs to strengthen this potential is probably obvious even for pacifists. The simplest, relatively inexpensive solution and, most importantly, the fastest to be achieved, is the establishment of a Territorial Defence Army, all the more so because there is great popularity in society, especially among young people, pro-defense behaviour. The management of the Ministry of National Defence immediately after taking over power decided to organize a new type of army, the Territorial Defence Army. It seemed that this idea would gain support among all the political forces in Parliament. I would like to believe that everyone in this Chamber wants a sovereign and strong Republic, and that is why I present such conviction here. Unfortunately, the debate on the contested law focused not on the formal assessment of the proposed solutions, but on the very idea of establishing a Territorial Defence Army. Representatives of some of the opposition tried to focus the debate on the imaginary objectives of organised territorial defence and alleged threats to a democratic state. No arguments were and are not in a position to change these arguments. Law and Justice believes that the Territorial Defence Army will significantly increase the dissuade power of the Armed Forces of the Republic of Poland. The draft law submitted optimally responds to the growing uncertainty about future military threats in our region. We want to assure our fellow countrymen that the Territorial Defence Army will increase the defence potential of Poland, and the training system of territorial defence soldiers will increase awareness of the responsibility of citizens for the security of the state and patriotism, especially among young people who will build the future of the Republic in a few years. Mr. Marshal! Parliamentary Club Law and Justice will support the bill submitted on the universal obligation to defend the Republic of Poland. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-15-sejm-30-1.u531	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Polish Free and Solidarnia Circle, I present my position on the draft law amending the Act on the universal obligation to defend the Republic of Poland and certain other laws, prints no. 966 and 1008. The Free and Solidarni Circle welcomes the initiative to build the Territorial Defence Army. In our view, this is an appropriate, strategic response to the exposure of military risks to the eastern direction. In the current geopolitical situation of the Republic of Poland, the only relatively fast, cheap and effective way to rebuild our military security is by appealing to the defensive potential of society. We are convinced of the operational capabilities of the Territorial Defence Army in carrying out anti-crisis and anti-terrorism activities, but above all, in our view, the Territorial Defence Army should become a key element of the country's defence system capable of carrying out technologically advanced military activities, alone and in cooperating with other military formations. In this context, it should be regarded as a legitimate minority amendment tabled by Ms Anna Maria Siarkowska, which extends the scope of the Commander of the Territorial Defence Army's operation by, quote: "executing tasks related to the participation of troops and divisions of the Territorial Defence Forces in the conduct of local protection and defence, protection and defence of strategic areas and facilities, and creating conditions for maneuvering and attacking the operational forces of their own and allies.' We also believe that the Territorial Defence Army system should be fully integrated into public administration systems for crisis management and public security and cooperate with all non-governmental organisations and be a fully professional formation based on local communities capable of taking on the most serious defence tasks. The latter aspect of local cooperation is extremely important, as we are currently dealing with new threats and ways of fighting in the world, e.g. Hybrid wars. At the same time, technological development makes the management of distributed systems based on online tools extremely efficient. Let me remind you that in the defence strategies of advanced countries there is a radical transformation in military techniques, e.g. The US arms program launched in 1999 strongly prefers unmanned aerial systems, i.e. drones. It is to be welcomed that similar initiatives have been taken by the Minister of National Defence, Mr Antoni Macierewicz. They should be commended. The actions taken confirm that Minister Macierewicz sees a new paradigm of defence based on theory and practice of developing dynamically dispersed military systems. They are based on remotely controlled automation and advanced IT technology. Territorial defence is becoming an essential link to such a modern defence system, and in this direction the country's defensive doctrine should be developed in order to obtain the maximum effect of repelling the potential aggressor in a minimal and short period of time. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The Free and Solidarnia Members' Circle will vote in favour of the bill submitted and the amendment of minorities tabled by the National Defence Committee. ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-15-sejm-30-1.u611	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! Today, at the meeting of the Committee on Public Finances, we adopted the report of the Finance Committee on the Government's draft law amending the Act on Taxation on Goods and Services and some other laws, print No 965 - the report is contained in print No 1035. We have examined the amendments, and in this context I would like to remind you that the European Commission estimates that the government of the Civic Platform and the PSL has created tax gaps in the state budget of PLN 40/60 billion. In the bill that we are working on today, the applicant, the government, introduces regulations that seal the VAT collection system. The recovery of at least 50% of the revenue from the reduction of this gap will support the implementation of the budget and will also provide a significant boost to the development of the economy. This draft amends the VAT Act, the Tax Code and the Tax Order. The VAT Act includes the method of tax settlement, the verification procedure system, the reverse charge mechanism and the buyer's joint and several liability for the tax arrears of the supplier of goods. In the Tax Code, the projector proposes tightening the sanctions. In the Tax Office it is proposed, among others. removing the possibility for certain VAT taxable persons to account for quarterly periods. Most of the amendments adopted by the Committee on Public Finance are sealing and clarifying. On the other hand, I would like to draw attention to the amendment introducing an extension of the deadline until 1 July 2017 for the entry into force of the regulation of the article. 1 point 3, i.e. the regulation leading to the removal from the scope of existing VAT exemptions of the exemption for services forming part of a tax-free financial or insurance service. In the current legal order such services as e.g. the elimination of damages provided by third parties to insurance undertakings has been exempt from VAT. Proposed provision in Article. Point 1 (3) excludes those entities from the exemption. Given that in the calculation process the insurance undertaking takes into account the incurred costs of services provided by third parties, it is appropriate to extend the date of entry into force of Article. Point 1 (3) to 1 July 2017 I would also like to draw attention to the amendment proposed for the article. Point 1 (23). This amendment extends the buyer's joint and several liability for the tax obligations of the supplier of rapeseed oil. On the market for transactions in which rapeseed oil is the subject, an extremely large number of transactions are carried out for undue financial benefits from undue VAT. The VAT extortion processer reduces tax revenues and, which is very important, storms fair competition on the market. Therefore, in order to eliminate this pathology on the market or at least to reduce its scale, the Committee on Public Finance has given a positive opinion on the preventive amendment. Following the adoption by the High Sejm of this amendment, the purchaser of rapeseed oil - the VAT taxable person will be jointly and severally liable with the supplier of the goods for his tax arrears in proportion to the volume of the purchase made. This record will mobilise the buyer to carefully verify the accuracy of the supplier of the goods before the transaction is made. Mrs. Marshal! High Seym! Minister! On behalf of the Committee on Public Finance, please accept the proposed amendments in accordance with the report of the Committee on Finance. Thank you very much.

These include the reverse load mechanism, including: for specific construction services, for the sale of processors, certain goods of gold, silver and platinum. In addition, the project amends the VAT accounting rules, introduces the obligation to submit electronic declarations, summary information for monthly periods. The bill, it must be said, was subject to consultation, but in a very short period, before submitting the proposal to the High House. Therefore, many questions have been raised from business organisations, as well as negative assessments, which highlight the absoluteness of some solutions, the increase in bureaucracy, the increase in labour expenditure and, of course, the increase in the cost of adapting companies to these new procedural requirements. The draft provides for a modification of the provisions on guarantee deposit and so-called deposit. joint and several liability. Additional conditions have been introduced to ensure that the buyer of the goods is exempt from joint and several liability. Additional requirements have been introduced that must be met so that the seller can lodge a guarantee deposit and be included in the list of entities that have lodged such a deposit. All the changes made will allow the guarantee deposit to be lodged and then remain on the list by sellers who ensure compliance with their tax obligations. The joint and several liability of the proxy has been introduced. The joint and several liability of the representative who filed the registration application, together with the registered taxable person, would concern the tax arrears arising from the activities carried out within 6 months from the date of registration of the taxable person as an active VAT taxable person. The conditions for refusing to register an entity as a VAT taxable person have been indicated and the conditions for removing a taxable person from the taxable person's register have been clarified. Numerous tax sanctions are foreseen for incorrect VAT clearance. Today, very numerous changes have been made to the work of the committee, mainly in a more precise way, proposed by both committee members and, very numerous, by the Legislative Bureau. It must be pointed out and made clear that only those amendments which were tabled by Members of the PIS have been taken into account and that the amendments proposed by the opposition have been rejected. In conclusion, High Izbo, I have to say with regret that the project under investigation is nothing more than the introduction of a tax on goods and services, not so much sealing and closing the tax gap as it complicates the forms and procedures of tax settlement for all entrepreneurs. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-11-15-sejm-30-1.u629	Thank you, Marshal. Despite the late season, I wanted to make a statement which, I think, is important for many Orava residents, who are affected by this statement, and especially the historical figure that worked among the Orava inhabitants. In October 2016, we celebrated the 74th anniversary of the death of Fr. Jan Góralik in Dachau camp. Fr. Jan Góralik was born on 13 June 1889 in the area of today's Staniątek near Niepołomic. He graduated from St. Anna in Cracow, where in 1907 he received a certificate of maturity. After graduating from junior high school, he began his studies at the Faculty of Theology at the Jagiellonian University in Cracow, where he received priestly ordination on 22 October 1911. He was a vicar in parishes in Rychwałd and Radochowa in Żywiecczyn, and after Poland regained independence in 1919 he started working in Cicin in the parish of St. Catherine. At this parish he was for 3 years to take the position of Vicar in 1922 in the parish of the Most Holy Savior in Krakow-Zwierzynec. He served there until 1927, when he was transferred to the pastoral service in Podwilk, Orava. Orava at that time was a unique place, special in free Poland. As every border area in the past was within different countries, starting from Poland, through Hungary, Austria-Hungary, then Czechoslovakia and Poland. Covering the parish of St. Marcin in Podwilk by Fr. Jan Góralik meant putting the parish under the jurisdiction of the Polish priest. From the first days of his activity Fr. Jan Góralik started intensive pastoral and social work in Podwilk. On his initiative, among others. was established on 7 October 1928. Kasa Stefczyk. On the basis of this cash register there is still a Cooperative Bank in Jabłonka, which cultivates memory of this well-deserved founder. The money worked successfully throughout the interwar period and supported many initiatives of Orava residents. In 1933 in the district of Nowytar, the Society for Public Schools was established and the first near the Society was created by Fr. Jan Góralik was founded, among others. in Podwilk. In 1938 Fr. Jan Góralik also became the councillor of the municipality of Jabłonka in order to effectively support the development of Podwilka and other towns of Orava. The Krakow Curia also appreciated the work of the priest, his efforts and achievements, making him Dean of Orava in 1930. Dramatic events on 1 September 1939, i.e. the crossing of the borders of Poland by German troops, caused that one of the first victims of World War II became Orava residents, including parish priest Fr. Jan Góralik and his vicars, who are arrested on September 2nd and transported to internment camps and later to concentration camps. After this arrest, Fr. Jan Góralik never returned to his parish again, was murdered in 1942 in Dachau. In this way Fr. Jan Góralik divided the fate of 3.5 thousand. Polish priests murdered by the invaders during World War II. The memory of this great priest, his testimony survived among the inhabitants. Today, commemorative plaques dedicated to his memory are located in the Cooperative Bank in Jabłonka and in the parish church of St. Marcin in Podwilk. The local middle school also bears its name - Fr. Jan Góralik. After many efforts, thanks to the work of Mr Łukasz Wiatr, a local historian, published a publication entitled: Ks. Jan Góralik. Priest, social worker, martyr, [[♪ The bell ♪]] which brings us closer to this unique figure, deserved for the whole Orava. Thank you very much.
